Quality Objectives\n\n| QO ID | Objective | Linked Business Objective | Target | Review Cadence |\n|-------|-----------|--------------------------|--------|---------------|\n| QO-ML-001 | Zero links quebrados | BO-ML-001 | 100% links `meli.la` reais | Weekly |\n| QO-ML-002 | Isolamento de atribui\u00e7\u00e3o | BO-ML-002 | 0 vazamentos cross-tenant | Monthly + por security-review |\n| QO-ML-003 | Receita rastre\u00e1vel | BO-ML-003 | `revenue_cents` reconcilia com postbacks | Monthly |\n| QO-ML-004 | Pagamento confi\u00e1vel | BO-ML-004 | \u2265 99% webhooks processados idempotentemente | Weekly |\n| QO-ML-005 | Disponibilidade | (transversal) | 99.5% uptime das fns ML/MP | Continuous |\n\n---\n\n## 2. KPI Dashboard Definition\n\n| KPI ID | Name | Formula | Target | Warn | Critical | Data Source |\n|--------|------|---------|--------|------|----------|-------------|\n| KPI-ML-001 | Real Link Rate | `count(short_url like 'meli.la%') / count(*)` | 100% | < 99% | < 90% | `affiliate_links` |\n| KPI-ML-002 | Broken Link Incidents | `count(short_url like '%panel.gcrux.com%' OR metadata->>'item_id' !~ '^MLB\\d{6,}$')` | 0 | \u2265 1 | \u2265 10 | `affiliate_links` |\n| KPI-ML-003 | Revenue Attribution | `count(distinct user_id where revenue_cents > 0)` | \u2265 1 | 0 (7d) | 0 (30d) | `affiliate_links` |\n| KPI-ML-004 | MP Webhook Success | `approved_processed / received` | \u2265 99% | < 95% | < 80% | `infra_health_logs` (mercado-pago) |\n| KPI-ML-005 | Edge Fn p95 Latency | `PERCENTILE_CONT(0.95)` | < 1500ms | 2000ms | 5000ms | Supabase logs |\n| KPI-ML-006 | mcoCoins Burn (links) | `sum(deductions) / active_users` | conforme plano | \u2014 | \u2014 | `profiles` + `mcoin_transactions` |\n\n---\n\n## 3. FMEA Table\n\n> Severity, Occurrence, Detection: 1 (best) \u2192 10 (worst). RPN = S \u00d7 O \u00d7 D. RPN > 100 requires action.\n\n| ID | Function | Failure Mode | Effect | S | O | D | RPN | Recommended Action |\n|----|---------|--------------|--------|---|---|---|-----|--------------------|\n| FMEA-ML-001 | Gera\u00e7\u00e3o de link (FR-ML-002) | Endpoint de afiliado errado \u2192 link inv\u00e1lido | Receita = 0; bug 404 persiste | 9 | 4 | 4 | **144** | OTD-ML-001: validar doc oficial ANTES de codar (Lei 1); E2E que afirma `meli.la` resolve 200 |\n| FMEA-ML-002 | Resolu\u00e7\u00e3o de credencial (FR-ML-003) | Cai em token global em vez de per-user | Atribui\u00e7\u00e3o vaza entre tenants; fraude | 9 | 3 | 5 | **135** | Resolu\u00e7\u00e3o per-user obrigat\u00f3ria + fail-closed 402 + RLS test cross-tenant + security-review |\n| FMEA-ML-003 | Webhook MP (FR-ML-009) | Assinatura n\u00e3o validada \u2192 pagamento forjado | Cr\u00e9dito falso; fraude financeira | 10 | 2 | 6 | **120** | Validar `x-signature` HMAC ANTES de processar; idempot\u00eancia por `payment.id` |\n| FMEA-ML-004 | Postback ML (FR-ML-005) | Replay de convers\u00e3o \u2192 double credit | Receita inflada/incorreta | 7 | 3 | 5 | **105** | Idempot\u00eancia por `order_id` (OTD-ML-004) |\n| FMEA-ML-005 | Token MP/ML (NFR-ML-006) | Token expira sem refresh | Fluxo quebra silenciosamente | 7 | 4 | 3 | 84 | Refresh autom\u00e1tico + pulse error + self-heal alert |\n| FMEA-ML-006 | mcoCoins (FR-ML-004) | RPC timeout \u2192 cobran\u00e7a dupla | Usu\u00e1rio cobrado 2x | 8 | 2 | 3 | 48 | Idempotency key na RPC |\n| FMEA-ML-007 | Rate limit ML | App suspenso por excesso | Indisponibilidade total | 8 | 2 | 4 | 64 | Backoff exp + cache de item + pulse degraded |\n| FMEA-ML-008 | Exposi\u00e7\u00e3o de secret | `client_secret`/`access_token` no bundle/log | Vazamento de credencial | 10 | 1 | 4 | 40 | Server-side only; bundle audit; nunca logar token |\n\n> **4 itens com RPN > 100** (FMEA-ML-001..004) \u2014 todos com a\u00e7\u00e3o recomendada definida.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Self-Optimization Loops\n\n| Loop ID | Trigger Condition | KPI Source | Autonomous Response | Escalation (human) |\n|---------|------------------|------------|--------------------|--------------------|\n| SOL-ML-001 | KPI-ML-002 > 0 (link quebrado detectado) | `affiliate_links` | Marca link como `degraded`; reprocessa via resolu\u00e7\u00e3o real | Alerta Sovereign se reprocesso falhar |\n| SOL-ML-002 | KPI-ML-005 lat\u00eancia > 2s por 5 chamadas | infra_health_logs | `mega-brain-daemon`: redeploy da edge fn | Alerta se > 5s |\n| SOL-ML-003 | KPI-ML-004 webhook success < 95% | infra_health_logs | Trigger `mp-payment-reconcile` cron antecipado | Alerta Sovereign |\n| SOL-ML-004 | Token MP expira em < 24h | `mercado_pago_config.expires_at` | Refresh autom\u00e1tico via refresh_token | Notificar user se refresh falhar |\n\n---\n\n## 5. Self-Healing Specifications\n\n### 5.1 Watchdog Telemetry Events\n\n| Event Type (`service` em infra_health_logs) | Trigger | Severity | Payload |\n|------------|---------|----------|---------|\n| `affiliate-link-resolution` (existe) | Gera\u00e7\u00e3o de link (healthy/degraded/error) | info/warn/error | `{ status }` |\n| `mercado-pago` (novo) | OAuth + webhook + payment | info/warn/error | `{ status }` |\n| `global-selling-sync` (novo) | Sync de cat\u00e1logo | info/warn/error | `{ status }` |\n\n### 5.2 Auto-Recovery Script Stub\n\n```bash\n#!/bin/bash\n# scripts/self-heal-mercado-livre-api.sh\n# Triggered by mega-brain-daemon when affiliate-link-resolution/mercado-pago error rate spikes\n\nfor FN in process-affiliate-link mercado-pago-webhook global-selling-sync; do\n  STATUS=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w \"%{http_code}\" \\\n    \"$SUPABASE_URL/functions/v1/$FN\" -X OPTIONS)\n  if [ \"$STATUS\" -ge 500 ]; then\n    echo \"[WARN] $FN unhealthy ($STATUS). Re-deploying...\"\n    npx supabase functions deploy \"$FN\"\n  fi\ndone\necho \"[OK] Self-heal sweep complete for mercado-livre-api\"\n```\n\n### 5.3 mega-brain-daemon Integration Points\n\n| Daemon Event | Condition | Action |\n|-------------|-----------|--------|\n| `health_check` | Watchdog cron `*/5` | Conta error rate de `affiliate-link-resolution`/`mercado-pago` |\n| `anomaly_detected` | error rate > 5/hora | Executa `self-heal-mercado-livre-api.sh` |\n| `recovery_confirmed` | health passa p\u00f3s-heal | Insere `recovery_ok` em `infra_health_logs` |\n\n---\n\n## 6. # BoK Agents Generator

Generates a full **8-agent AI system** grounded in enterprise Bodies of Knowledge, plus the three
cross-agent layers that make them interoperable as a living system.

**System Identity — always include this block at the top of every generated document:**
```
# SOFTWARE ENGINEERING INTELLIGENCE
## KNOWLEDGE CORE v2.0.0 | STATUS: ONLINE

> Integrated Body of Knowledge for Engineering, Architecture, Delivery,
> Security, Experience and Product Excellence
>
> **OUTCOMES:** Better Knowledge · Better Decisions · Better Outcomes
> **ONE KNOWLEDGE ECOSYSTEM. INFINITE POSSIBILITIES.**
```

**System Footer — always include this block at the end of every generated document:**
```
---
→ ALIGN  →  INTEGRATE  →  OPTIMIZE  →  INNOVATE  →  TRANSFORM  →  DELIVER VALUE

**SYSTEM STATUS: ALL SYSTEMS OPERATIONAL**
```

---

## Execution Flow

### Step 1 — Context Capture

Before generating, check if the user provided any of these optional context inputs:

| Input | Examples |
|---|---|
| **Industry** | Fintech, Healthcare, Retail, Manufacturing, SaaS |
| **Company maturity** | Startup, Scale-up, Mid-market, Enterprise |
| **Digital capabilities** | Low / Medium / High / AI-native |
| **Business model** | B2B, B2C, B2B2C, Marketplace, Platform |

If no context is provided → generate the **generic enterprise-ready version** (explicitly note this at the top of the output).

If context is provided → adapt every agent's Use Cases, Tools, and KPIs to that context.

---

### Step 2 — Generate All 8 BoK Agents

Generate agents in this exact order. Each agent uses the **strict output format** below.

**Agent order:**
1. BABOK — Business Analysis
2. EABOK — Enterprise Architecture
3. DMBOK — Data Management
4. BPM CBOK — Business Process Management
5. SWEBOK — Software Engineering
6. CISSP CBK — Cybersecurity
7. CXBOK — Customer Experience
8. ProdBOK — Product Management

---

### Strict Output Format Per Agent

```
---

🤖 Agent: [BoK Name] — [Domain Label]

### 1. Purpose
[2–3 sentence strategic description. What this agent owns in a business context. NOT a textbook definition.]

### 2. Core Capabilities
[6–10 capabilities directly derived from the BoK. Noun phrases, not verbs.]

### 3. Skills (Operational Level)
[Bullet list. Action-oriented. Each skill starts with a verb. Map to the capabilities above.]

### 4. Inputs
[What this agent needs to operate: data, documents, signals, systems, events.]

### 5. Outputs
[Deliverables, insights, decisions, artifacts this agent produces.]

### 6. Decision Scope
[What decisions this agent can autonomously support or recommend. Be specific about autonomy level.]

### 7. Tools & Integrations
[Platforms, systems, APIs this agent typically connects to. Real tool names.]

### 8. KPIs / Success Metrics
[Business + operational metrics. Mix leading and lagging indicators.]

### 9. Example Use Cases
[3 real-world scenarios. Format: **Scenario Title** — 2-sentence description of the problem + value created.]

### 10. Collaboration Map
[How this agent interfaces with other BoK agents. Format: → AgentName: [nature of dependency or data flow]]
```

---

### Step 3 — Cross-Agent Layers

After all 8 agents, generate the three cross-agent sections:

#### 3a. Orchestration Model

```
---

## 🔄 Orchestration Model

Describe the end-to-end flow: how agents activate each other, share outputs, and form closed loops.
Include:
- Which agent is the primary orchestrator (or if orchestration is event-driven)
- Trigger conditions (what activates the system)
- Key handoff points between agents
- Feedback loops
- Conflict resolution (when two agents have overlapping scope)
```

#### 3b. Intelligent Engineering Layer

```
---

## 🧠 Intelligent Engineering Layer

Explain how the system integrates across SEVEN dimensions (as defined in the Knowledge Core):

| Dimension | Description |
|---|---|
| **People** | How agents augment human roles and teams |
| **Process** | How agents embed into business workflows |
| **Technology** | Infrastructure and platform requirements |
| **Data** | Data flows, quality gates, and knowledge graph |
| **Knowledge** | How agents learn, update, and share knowledge |
| **Value** | How the system translates agent outputs into business value |
| **Sustainable Impact** | How the system generates outcomes that endure — environmental, social, and economic sustainability of the decisions made by the agent ecosystem |
```

#### 3c. Cross-Cutting Enablers

```
---

## ⚙️ Cross-Cutting Enablers

Define how these five capabilities are embedded across ALL agents (not per-agent — system-wide):

1. **Systems Thinking** — [How the system models interdependencies]
2. **Analytics & Metrics** — [Shared observability layer]
3. **Collaboration & Communication** — [Inter-agent and human-agent protocols]
4. **Automation & Tooling** — [Shared automation infrastructure]
5. **Ethics, Compliance & Sustainability** — [Governance model across agents]
```

---

## Quality Rules

Apply these rules during generation — do NOT repeat them in the output:

- **No textbook definitions** — every section must describe execution, not theory
- **No capability repetition** across agents unless absolutely necessary (flag it when it occurs)
- **Tools must be real** — no invented platform names
- **Collaboration Map must form a coherent graph** — every agent must connect to at least 3 others
- **Use Cases must be industry-specific** if context was provided
- **KPIs must be measurable** — no vague metrics like "improved quality"
- **Decision Scope must distinguish** between autonomous recommendation vs. human-in-the-loop

---

## Output — Write to File (Primary Delivery Mode)

This skill runs in **Claude Code (terminal)**. Output is always written to disk as a `.md` file.
Do NOT print the full content to the terminal — write the file, then confirm to the user.

### File naming convention
```
bok-agents-system[--<slug>].md
```
- No context provided → `bok-agents-system.md`
- Context provided → slugify the industry + maturity, e.g. `bok-agents-system--fintech-enterprise.md`

### Output path
Write to the **current working directory** unless the user specifies otherwise.
If a path is specified, respect it exactly.

### Write procedure (execute in this order)
1. Compose the full document in memory following Steps 1–3
2. Write to file using the bash tool or Python `open()` — single write operation
3. Print a short confirmation to the terminal:
   ```
   ✅ BoK Agents System written to: ./bok-agents-system--<slug>.md
   📄 Agents: 8 | Sections: 10 per agent + 3 cross-agent layers
   📁 Size: ~<N> KB
   ```
4. Do NOT print the document content to stdout

### Document structure (inside the .md file)
- H1 title: `# BoK Agents System — [Context or "Enterprise-Ready Generic Version"]`
- If context was provided: frontmatter metadata block at the top:
  ```markdown
  > **Context applied:** Industry: X | Maturity: Y | Digital capabilities: Z | Model: W
  ```
- Use `##` for each agent header (`## 🤖 Agent: BABOK — Business Analysis`)
- Use `###` for the 10 section headers within each agent
- Use `---` as horizontal rule between agents
- Use `##` for the three cross-agent layer headers
- Emoji headers exactly as specified (🤖, 🔄, 🧠, ⚙️)

### Context adaptation rules (when context is provided)
Adapt these fields per agent — everything else stays structurally consistent:
- **Use Cases** → scenarios specific to the provided industry and maturity level
- **Tools & Integrations** → prioritize tools common in that industry/stack
- **KPIs** → benchmark values calibrated to maturity level (startup vs. enterprise targets differ)
- **Decision Scope** → autonomy level adjusted to digital capability maturity (Low = more human-in-loop)

Do NOT adapt: agent Purpose, Core Capabilities, Skills structure, or the cross-agent layers topology.

---

## Reference: BoK Domain Map

See `references/bok-domains.md` for the authoritative list of capabilities per BoK, used to
validate that generated capabilities are grounded in the actual body of knowledge (not invented).
Read this file if you need to verify correctness of a specific BoK's scope.

Tools & Integrations\nSupabase (schema + Edge Functions runtime), Docker Sovereign Mesh, ChromaDB, n8n (Global Selling sync), Knowledge Mesh (`mcorch_nodes`/`mcorch_edges`), diagramas (Mermaid/ASCII no SDD).\n\n### 8. KPIs / Success Metrics\n% de capacidades reusadas vs recriadas; n\u00ba de viola\u00e7\u00f5es do API Tenancy Model barradas em design (target 100% barradas); ader\u00eancia ao SDD (desvios/sprint); tempo de transi\u00e7\u00e3o entre gates de m\u00f3dulo.\n\n### 9. Example Use Cases\n- **Reuso vs recriar** \u2014 em vez de nova fun\u00e7\u00e3o de afiliado, estende `process-affiliate-link` (j\u00e1 resolve item via `/sites/MLB/search`), economizando blast radius.\n- **Roadmap de moeda forte** \u2014 sequencia Global Selling (M\u00f3dulo B) como Future, dependente de valida\u00e7\u00e3o do MVP, evitando over-engineering precoce.\n- **Padr\u00e3o de credencial** \u2014 imp\u00f5e que `mercado_pago_config` espelhe a coluna-level security de `social_credentials`.\n\n### 10. Collaboration Map\n- \u2192 SWEBOK: entrega o blueprint de componentes para constru\u00e7\u00e3o\n- \u2192 DMBOK: alinha schema de `mercado_pago_config` e isolamento de tenant\n- \u2192 CISSP CBK: co-desenha a fronteira de confian\u00e7a (server-side only)\n- \u2192 ProdBOK: traduz roadmap de capacidade em release phasing\n\n---\n\n## \ud83e\udd16 Agent: DMBOK \u2014 Data Management\n\n### 1. Purpose\nDono dos dados de credencial, link e receita: garante que `affiliate_links.short_url` nunca contenha `panel.gcrux.com` nem ID MLB fabricado, que `mercado_pago_config` viva sob RLS `auth.uid()`, e que a linhagem de atribui\u00e7\u00e3o de receita (convers\u00e3o \u2192 tenant) seja rastre\u00e1vel e \u00e0 prova de replay.\n\n### 2. Core Capabilities\n- Data Quality Management (regra: `short_url` come\u00e7a com `meli.la`; `item_id` regex `^MLB\\d{6,}$`)\n- Data Security (column-level em `access_token`/`client_secret`; RLS default-deny)\n- Reference & Master Data (`vm_affiliate_products` como master de cat\u00e1logo, +`currency`)\n- Data Integration (postback ML \u2192 `revenue_cents`; webhook MP \u2192 status)\n- Metadata Management (`affiliate_links.metadata` JSONB: item_id, resolved_url, self_healed_from)\n- Data Lineage (convers\u00e3o \u2192 `ATTRIBUTES_REVENUE_TO` edge \u2192 tenant node)\n- Tenant Isolation Map governance (user_id + project_id scope por tabela)\n\n### 3. Skills (Operational Level)\n- Definir e fiscalizar CHECK/valida\u00e7\u00e3o que rejeita link n\u00e3o-`meli.la`\n- Garantir UNIQUE(user_id) em `mercado_pago_config` e UNIQUE(user_id, platform) em `affiliate_config`\n- Modelar linhagem de receita query\u00e1vel (n\u00e3o s\u00f3 declarativa em metadata)\n- Aplicar pol\u00edtica de reten\u00e7\u00e3o (LGPD: credenciais entram no `delete_account()` RPC)\n- Auditar `vm_affiliate_products` para detectar IDs MLB inv\u00e1lidos/inativos\n\n### 4. Inputs\nData model `06-data-model.md`, schema real Supabase, payloads de postback ML/MP, cat\u00e1logo `vm_affiliate_products`, logs de qualidade.\n\n### 5. Outputs\nMigrations com RLS+\u00edndices, scorecards de qualidade de dados, mapa de linhagem de receita, pol\u00edtica de reten\u00e7\u00e3o/LGPD, regras de valida\u00e7\u00e3o por coluna.\n\n### 6. Decision Scope\n**Autonomous recommendation:** regras de qualidade, \u00edndices, detec\u00e7\u00e3o de IDs inv\u00e1lidos. **Human-in-the-loop:** migration nova (passa `/security-review`), pol\u00edtica de reten\u00e7\u00e3o, exclus\u00e3o LGPD.\n\n### 7. Tools & Integrations\nSupabase Postgres (RLS, pgvector, HNSW), `affiliate_config`/`affiliate_links`/`vm_affiliate_products`/`mercado_pago_config`, `embed-mcorch-node`, `scripts/backfill-embeddings.ts`, REST PostgREST.\n\n### 8. KPIs / Success Metrics\nKPI-ML-001 Real Link Rate = 100%; KPI-ML-002 Broken Link Incidents = 0; % linhas de cat\u00e1logo com MLB v\u00e1lido/ativo; 0 vazamentos cross-tenant em auditoria RLS; % convers\u00f5es com linhagem completa.\n\n### 9. Example Use Cases\n- **Quality gate de link** \u2014 rejeita INSERT em `affiliate_links` cujo `short_url` aponte para `panel.gcrux.com` (KPI-ML-002 \u2192 0).\n- **Cat\u00e1logo verificado** \u2014 sinaliza IDs MLB inativos em `vm_affiliate_products` antes que virem link 404 (o exato sintoma do Afiliados bug 1).\n- **Linhagem de receita audit\u00e1vel** \u2014 torna a atribui\u00e7\u00e3o query\u00e1vel via edge `ATTRIBUTES_REVENUE_TO`, n\u00e3o s\u00f3 declarada.\n\n### 10. Collaboration Map\n- \u2192 CISSP CBK: co-define column-level security em tokens\n- \u2192 BPM CBOK: fornece o ponto de persist\u00eancia de cada processo\n- \u2192 EABOK: implementa o schema dentro da topologia aprovada\n- \u2192 SWEBOK: entrega contratos de dados (valida\u00e7\u00e3o) para as edge functions\n\n---\n\n## \ud83e\udd16 Agent: BPM CBOK \u2014 Business Process Management\n\n### 1. Purpose\nDono dos 5 processos da integra\u00e7\u00e3o (PROC-ML-001..005): orquestra a gera\u00e7\u00e3o de link, o postback de convers\u00e3o, o OAuth MP, o webhook de pagamento e o sync Global Selling \u2014 cada um com gateway de falha expl\u00edcito, backoff e SLA. Garante que o passo 5 do `orchestrate-content` degrade graciosamente (publica sem link em vez de link 404).\n\n### 2. Core Capabilities\n- Process Modeling (BPMN-lite dos 5 PROC-ML em swimlanes)\n- Process Design (gateways de credencial/saldo/item; caminhos de erro)\n- Process Performance Management (SLA p95 por processo)\n- Process Automation (n8n Global Selling sync; cron mp-payment-reconcile)\n- Process Governance (idempot\u00eancia obrigat\u00f3ria; preservar crons existentes)\n- Exception/Retry orchestration (backoff exponencial 2/4/8s)\n\n### 3. Skills (Operational Level)\n- Desenhar o gateway \"credencial per-user existe?\" como fail-closed (402), n\u00e3o fallback global\n- Especificar idempot\u00eancia por `order_id` (postback) e `payment.id` (MP webhook)\n- Definir degrada\u00e7\u00e3o graciosa no step 5 do `orchestrate-content` (nunca publicar link quebrado)\n- Orquestrar reconcilia\u00e7\u00e3o de pagamentos pendentes via cron `*/30`\n- Garantir ACK r\u00e1pido (<300ms) no webhook MP para evitar re-tentativa do provedor\n\n### 4. Inputs\nProcess flow `07-process-flow.md`, defini\u00e7\u00f5es de SLA, cat\u00e1logo de crons existentes, status de execu\u00e7\u00e3o das edge functions.\n\n### 5. Outputs\nMapas BPMN-lite, blueprints n8n com ramo de erro, defini\u00e7\u00f5es de cron idempotentes, matriz de retry/backoff, especifica\u00e7\u00e3o de SLA por processo.\n\n### 6. Decision Scope\n**Autonomous recommendation:** retry/backoff, agendamento de cron, ponto de degrada\u00e7\u00e3o graciosa. **Human-in-the-loop:** novo cron em produ\u00e7\u00e3o, mudan\u00e7a de SLA contratual, altera\u00e7\u00e3o do pipeline `orchestrate-content`.\n\n### 7. Tools & Integrations\nn8n, Supabase Edge Functions, cron (crontab gcrux/ubuntu), `infra_health_logs`, `orchestrate-content` pipeline, Guardian/Telegram (alertas).\n\n### 8. KPIs / Success Metrics\nPROC-ML-001 p95 < 1.5s; PROC-ML-004 ack < 300ms; KPI-ML-004 MP Webhook Success \u2265 99%; % processos com ramo de erro definido = 100%; n\u00ba de links 404 publicados (target 0 via degrada\u00e7\u00e3o graciosa).\n\n### 9. Example Use Cases\n- **Degrada\u00e7\u00e3o graciosa** \u2014 quando a API de afiliados falha, o step 5 publica o conte\u00fado sem link em vez de um `meli.la` quebrado, preservando a campanha.\n- **Reconcilia\u00e7\u00e3o de pagamento** \u2014 cron `mp-payment-reconcile` recupera notifica\u00e7\u00f5es MP perdidas idempotentemente.\n- **Fail-closed na credencial** \u2014 gateway retorna 402 imediato quando `affiliate_config` ausente, sem cair em token global.\n\n### 10. Collaboration Map\n- \u2192 SWEBOK: especifica o comportamento que a edge function implementa\n- \u2192 DMBOK: define onde cada passo persiste estado\n- \u2192 CISSP CBK: posiciona a valida\u00e7\u00e3o de assinatura do webhook como primeiro gateway\n- \u2192 CXBOK: traduz caminhos de erro em mensagens PT-BR para o usu\u00e1rio\n\n---\n\n## \ud83e\udd16 Agent: SWEBOK \u2014 Software Engineering\n\n### 1. Purpose\nConstr\u00f3i e mant\u00e9m as edge functions da integra\u00e7\u00e3o sob Harness Engineering: estende `process-affiliate-link` para emitir `meli.la` real, cria `mercado-pago-oauth`/`webhook`/`global-selling-sync`, e implementa OTD-ML-001 SOMENTE ap\u00f3s valida\u00e7\u00e3o material do endpoint oficial. \u00c9 quem transforma FR-ML em c\u00f3digo test\u00e1vel \u2014 nunca antes do BoK Gate.\n\n### 2. Core Capabilities\n- Software Construction (Deno Edge Functions, hooks TanStack Query display-only)\n- Software Testing (Unit/Integration/E2E por FR-ML; 199 testes baseline)\n- Software Design (contratos de API, JWT guard, mcoCoins RPC point)\n- Configuration Management (deploy versionado de edge functions)\n- Software Quality (NFR-ML: p95, complexidade ciclom\u00e1tica \u2264 10)\n- Software Engineering Economics (custo de chamada externa vs cache)\n- API design (ML `/items`, afiliados, MP OAuth/preferences)\n\n### 3. Skills (Operational Level)\n- Estender `process-affiliate-link` para resolver item real + gerar `meli.la` (ap\u00f3s OTD-ML-001)\n- Remover IDs MLB hardcoded de `link-forge.ts` (FR-ML-006) substituindo por resolu\u00e7\u00e3o real\n- Implementar `deduct_mco_coins` como ponto at\u00f4mico antes da chamada externa\n- Escrever E2E que AFIRMA materialmente que o link resolve 200 (n\u00e3o `--bypass`)\n- Implementar backoff exponencial em rate limit ML/MP (NFR-ML-007)\n\n### 4. Inputs\nFRD `04-frd.md`, SDD `05-sdd.md`, c\u00f3digo existente (`process-affiliate-link`, `link-forge.ts`, `handle-ml-postback`), resultado material da valida\u00e7\u00e3o de OTD-ML-001.\n\n### 5. Outputs\nEdge functions deployadas (com tamanho/proof), hooks, su\u00edte de testes verde (sem bypass), PRs rastreados a FR-ML, deploy logs materiais.\n\n### 6. Decision Scope\n**Autonomous recommendation:** estrutura de c\u00f3digo, estrat\u00e9gia de teste, refactor. **Human-in-the-loop:** deploy em produ\u00e7\u00e3o, qualquer uso de flag que pule verifica\u00e7\u00e3o (ex.: `--bypass` exige aprova\u00e7\u00e3o Sovereign + asterisco no seal).\n\n### 7. Tools & Integrations\nSupabase CLI (`supabase functions deploy`), Deno, Vitest, Playwright/agent-browser (E2E), Bun, ESLint, Git, OpenRouter, ML/MP REST APIs.\n\n### 8. KPIs / Success Metrics\nTestes verdes sem bypass (199/199); NFR-ML-001 p95 < 1500ms; deploy size registrado por fun\u00e7\u00e3o; 0 secrets no bundle (NFR-ML-006); cobertura de teste por FR-ML cr\u00edtico = 100%.\n\n### 9. Example Use Cases\n- **OTD-ML-001 first, code second** \u2014 n\u00e3o implementa gera\u00e7\u00e3o de link at\u00e9 confirmar materialmente o endpoint oficial em developers.mercadolivre.com.br (evita queimar cr\u00e9ditos em tentativa-erro).\n- **E2E honesto** \u2014 escreve o teste que afirma `meli.la` resolve 200; recusa `--bypass` como prova de sucesso (Lei 1).\n- **Remo\u00e7\u00e3o de placeholders** \u2014 substitui o hardcode MLB em `link-forge.ts` por resolu\u00e7\u00e3o via `/items/{id}`.\n\n### 10. Collaboration Map\n- \u2192 BABOK: consome FR-ML + AC; reporta viabilidade de OTD-ML-001\n- \u2192 CISSP CBK: implementa JWT guard, signature validation, fail-closed\n- \u2192 BPM CBOK: codifica os gateways e retries dos processos\n- \u2192 DMBOK: aplica os contratos de valida\u00e7\u00e3o de dados nas entradas\n\n---\n\n## \ud83e\udd16 Agent: CISSP CBK \u2014 Cybersecurity\n\n### 1. Purpose\nDono do perfil financeiro elevado: impede pagamento forjado (webhook MP sem assinatura validada \u2014 FMEA-ML-003 RPN 120), vazamento de atribui\u00e7\u00e3o entre tenants (token global em vez de per-user \u2014 FMEA-ML-002 RPN 135) e exposi\u00e7\u00e3o de tokens. Trata a integra\u00e7\u00e3o como superf\u00edcie financeira, n\u00e3o como feature comum.\n\n### 2. Core Capabilities\n- Software Development Security (fail-closed credential resolution; server-side tokens)\n- Identity & Access Management (RLS `auth.uid()`, JWT validation, RESTRICTIVE no-delete)\n- Security Architecture (STRIDE com 8 amea\u00e7as; fronteira de confian\u00e7a server-side)\n- Security & Risk Management (BR-RISK-ML; RPN dos FMEA financeiros)\n- Cryptography (valida\u00e7\u00e3o HMAC `x-signature` do webhook MP)\n- Security Assessment (`/security-review` obrigat\u00f3rio em cada migration)\n- Compliance mapping (LGPD; PCI-adjacent \u2014 n\u00e3o armazenar dados de cart\u00e3o)\n\n### 3. Skills (Operational Level)\n- Validar `x-signature` HMAC ANTES de processar qualquer notifica\u00e7\u00e3o MP (FMEA-ML-003)\n- Impor resolu\u00e7\u00e3o per-user fail-closed e barrar qualquer `Deno.env.get('<SERVICE>_API_KEY')` em fluxo user-facing (FMEA-ML-002)\n- Garantir idempot\u00eancia por `order_id`/`payment.id` (anti-replay, FMEA-ML-004)\n- Aplicar column-level security em `access_token`/`client_secret`; nunca logar token\n- Conduzir threat modeling STRIDE em cada edge function nova\n\n### 4. Inputs\nSDD \u00a75 (security design), payloads de webhook, secrets inventory (vault key names), pol\u00edticas RLS, resultados de `/security-review`.\n\n### 5. Outputs\nPol\u00edticas RLS revisadas, parecer de threat model, gate de `/security-review`, runbook de incidente (token roubado), checklist de compliance LGPD/financial.\n\n### 6. Decision Scope\n**Autonomous recommendation:** pol\u00edticas RLS, regras de valida\u00e7\u00e3o de assinatura, classifica\u00e7\u00e3o de risco. **Human-in-the-loop / VETO:** qualquer fluxo que vaze credencial cross-tenant ou processe pagamento sem assinatura validada \u2014 **bloqueia o deploy** (n\u00e3o \u00e9 recomenda\u00e7\u00e3o, \u00e9 veto).\n\n### 7. Tools & Integrations\nSupabase RLS + Vault, `has_role()` SECURITY DEFINER, `/security-review` skill, `infra_health_logs` (eventos de seguran\u00e7a), HMAC libs (Deno), pre-commit anti-credential hook.\n\n### 8. KPIs / Success Metrics\nNFR-ML-008 vazamentos cross-tenant = 0; FMEA-ML-002/003 RPN reduzido p\u00f3s-mitiga\u00e7\u00e3o; % migrations com `/security-review` = 100%; webhooks com assinatura inv\u00e1lida rejeitados = 100%; 0 tokens em logs/bundle.\n\n### 9. Example Use Cases\n- **Pagamento forjado barrado** \u2014 webhook MP com `x-signature` inv\u00e1lida \u00e9 rejeitado e gera pulse `error` para investiga\u00e7\u00e3o de fraude.\n- **Veto de token global** \u2014 bloqueia PR que resolveria afiliado via env global em fluxo user-facing (regress\u00e3o do que v6.14.0 removeu).\n- **Anti-replay** \u2014 idempot\u00eancia por `order_id` impede double-credit de comiss\u00e3o.\n\n### 10. Collaboration Map\n- \u2192 SWEBOK: fornece os guards que o c\u00f3digo implementa (veto power)\n- \u2192 DMBOK: co-define column-level security e RLS\n- \u2192 BPM CBOK: posiciona signature validation como primeiro gateway do webhook\n- \u2192 BABOK: tra\u00e7a BR-RISK-ML para requisitos de mitiga\u00e7\u00e3o\n\n---\n\n## \ud83e\udd16 Agent: CXBOK \u2014 Customer Experience\n\n### 1. Purpose\nDono da experi\u00eancia do criador-afiliado: garante que a jornada config\u2192gerar\u2192converter\u2192ROI seja transparente e que toda falha vire mensagem PT-BR acion\u00e1vel (nunca falha silenciosa). Transforma o fail-closed 402 de erro t\u00e9cnico em orienta\u00e7\u00e3o (\"Configure suas credenciais em /dashboard/affiliates\").\n\n### 2. Core Capabilities\n- Journey Management (jornada do criador + jornada de pagamento MP)\n- Experience Design (estados de credencial ausente, link gerado, convers\u00e3o)\n- Voice of the Customer (feedback dogfood do Sovereign como sinal prim\u00e1rio)\n- Measurement & Analytics (ativa\u00e7\u00e3o MP, taxa de gera\u00e7\u00e3o de link bem-sucedida)\n- Experience Design para onboarding (OAuth Connect MP sem fric\u00e7\u00e3o t\u00e9cnica)\n- Omnichannel orchestration (toast, badge \ud83d\udcb0 ML, banner inline)\n\n### 3. Skills (Operational Level)\n- Converter HTTP 402/422 em mensagens PT-BR acion\u00e1veis com deep-link\n- Desenhar o estado \"credencial ausente\" como banner inline, n\u00e3o erro gen\u00e9rico\n- Mapear a jornada de OAuth MP minimizando passos t\u00e9cnicos para Persona 2\n- Definir feedback visual de sucesso (badge \ud83d\udcb0 ML emerald ap\u00f3s monetizar)\n- Instrumentar pontos de fric\u00e7\u00e3o na ativa\u00e7\u00e3o (drop-off no OAuth)\n\n### 4. Inputs\nPRD `03-prd.md` (personas P1/P2, journey maps), c\u00f3digos de erro do FRD, feedback dogfood, m\u00e9tricas de ativa\u00e7\u00e3o.\n\n### 5. Outputs\nEspecifica\u00e7\u00e3o de microcopy PT-BR, blueprint de jornada, mapa de touchpoints, defini\u00e7\u00f5es de estado de UI (loading/erro/sucesso), relat\u00f3rio de fric\u00e7\u00e3o de onboarding.\n\n### 6. Decision Scope\n**Autonomous recommendation:** microcopy, estados de UI, fluxo de onboarding. **Human-in-the-loop:** mudan\u00e7a de jornada que afete convers\u00e3o, decis\u00f5es de marca (uso do `--ml-yellow`).\n\n### 7. Tools & Integrations\nReact + Tailwind (display-only components), `sonner` (toasts), TanStack Query (estados), `/dashboard/affiliates` + `/dashboard/settings`, AffiliateProductsPage, design tokens (PRD \u00a76).\n\n### 8. KPIs / Success Metrics\nTaxa de conclus\u00e3o de OAuth MP (ativa\u00e7\u00e3o); CSAT do criador; % de erros que exibem mensagem acion\u00e1vel = 100%; tempo at\u00e9 primeiro link gerado; drop-off no onboarding < alvo.\n\n### 9. Example Use Cases\n- **Fail-closed humano** \u2014 em vez de \"402\", o criador v\u00ea \"Configure suas credenciais de afiliado em /dashboard/affiliates\" com bot\u00e3o.\n- **Onboarding MP de baixa fric\u00e7\u00e3o** \u2014 Persona 2 conecta Mercado Pago em 1 clique OAuth, sem colar token manualmente.\n- **Feedback de valor** \u2014 badge \ud83d\udcb0 ML aparece ao monetizar, fechando o loop emocional de \"deu certo\".\n\n### 10. Collaboration Map\n- \u2192 ProdBOK: alinha jornada a m\u00e9tricas de ativa\u00e7\u00e3o e reten\u00e7\u00e3o\n- \u2192 BPM CBOK: recebe os caminhos de erro dos processos para virar microcopy\n- \u2192 BABOK: fornece sinais de VoC (dogfood) que viram requisitos\n- \u2192 SWEBOK: especifica estados de UI que os hooks display-only refletem\n\n---\n\n## \ud83e\udd16 Agent: ProdBOK \u2014 Product Management\n\n### 1. Purpose\nDono do release phasing e da economia: sequencia M\u00f3dulo A (MVP) \u2192 C (v1.0) \u2192 B (Future), define a precifica\u00e7\u00e3o em mcoCoins (1/link), e conecta a integra\u00e7\u00e3o ao Million-BRL Roadmap (Link Forge). Decide o que N\u00c3O fazer agora (Global Selling, Envios) tanto quanto o que fazer.\n\n### 2. Skills (Operational Level)\n- Definir o success gate do MVP (1 `meli.la` real por `auth.uid()` resolvendo 200)\n- Precificar gera\u00e7\u00e3o de link em mcoCoins alinhado aos tiers de plano\n- Sequenciar Global Selling como Future condicionado \u00e0 demanda de moeda forte\n- Conectar KPI-ML-003 (revenue attribution) ao Million-BRL Roadmap\n- Decidir Won't-Have expl\u00edcito (Mercado Envios) e comunicar o porqu\u00ea\n\n### 4. Inputs\nBRD `02-brd.md` (BO-ML, ROI, mcoCoins economics), PRD (MoSCoW, release phasing), m\u00e9tricas de receita reais (`affiliate_links.revenue_cents`), sprint priorities.\n\n### 5. Telemetria `infra_health_logs` fecha o loop auton\u00f4mico (mega-brain-daemon \u2192 self-heal).\n- **Conflict resolution:** quando escopo se sobrep\u00f5e (ex.: DMBOK quer \u00edndice, CISSP quer restri\u00e7\u00e3o), **CISSP CBK vence em decis\u00f5es de seguran\u00e7a financeira**; **EABOK vence em decis\u00f5es de topologia**; **ProdBOK + Sovereign vencem em trade-off de escopo/prazo**. > Aqui estão os resultados da busca sobre o programa de afiliados do Mercado Livre e APIs para gerar links de afiliado:

> ## Resultados principais:
> 
> Se você tem um blog, comparador de preços ou bot de Telegram com alto volume de links, pode usar a API de afiliados do ML para gerar links programaticamente, com documentação oficial disponível no Developer Portal do Mercado Livre.
> 
> **Métodos para gerar links de afiliado:**
> 
> 1. **Portal do Afiliado (Método manual):** Acesse mercadolivre.com.br/l/afiliados-home, faça login com sua conta aprovada, cole a URL do produto que deseja divulgar, selecione o canal de divulgação cadastrado e clique em "Gerar link" — o sistema retorna a URL rastreada com seu ID de afiliado.
> 
> 2. **API de Afiliados:** A API de afiliados do ML permite gerar links programaticamente para quem tem alto volume de links, sendo recomendada para afiliados que já geram mais de 500 cliques por dia.
> 
> 3. **Barra de Afiliados:** Com a Barra de Afiliados, você pode gerar seus links de maneira prática, tanto pelo computador, quanto pelo celular.
> 
> **Informações sobre validade dos links:**
> 
> O link em si não expira, porém se o produto for removido da plataforma, o link retornará erro 404, e a janela de atribuição da comissão é de 30 dias a partir do clique.
> 
> Os resultados mostram que há soluções de terceiros (como no GitHub) que oferecem APIs para gerar links de afiliado, mas a documentação oficial está no Developer Portal do Mercado Livre.

> Based on the search results, here's what I found regarding the Mercado Libre affiliate program API and affiliate link generation:
> 
> ## Key Finding: No Dedicated Affiliate API
> 
> The Mercado Libre Affiliate Program does not offer a dedicated public API for affiliate-specific functions, such as searching products, identifying promotional offers, generating affiliate links, or automating other affiliate tasks.
> 
> ## How Affiliate Links Are Generated
> 
> Affiliate link generation and management occur exclusively through the official affiliate dashboard or platform after registration and profile activation, requiring manual processes rather than programmatic access.
> 
> ## General API Documentation Available
> 
> While there's no affiliate-specific API, the general Mercado Libre API supports programmatic product searches through endpoints like /sites/{site_id}/search with filters for categories, prices, or conditions, which affiliates may use for manual product discovery prior to promotion.
> 
> You can access general Mercado Libre developer documentation at the official developer portal (https://developers.mercadolibre.com), but for affiliate link generation specifically, you'll need to use the official affiliate dashboard rather than an API.
>

> 186	  }
> 187	
> 188	  const { data: assets, error } = await supabase
> 189	    .from("mcorch_nodes")

# firecrawl scrape

Scrape one or more URLs. Returns clean, LLM-optimized markdown. Multiple URLs are scraped concurrently.

## When to use

- You have a specific URL and want its content
- The page is static or JS-rendered (SPA)
- Step 2 in the [workflow escalation pattern](firecrawl-cli): search → **scrape** → map → crawl → interact

## Quick start

```bash
# Basic markdown extraction
firecrawl scrape "<url>" -o .firecrawl/page.md

# Main content only, no nav/footer
firecrawl scrape "<url>" --only-main-content -o .firecrawl/page.md

# Wait for JS to render, then scrape
firecrawl scrape "<url>" --wait-for 3000 -o .firecrawl/page.md

# Multiple URLs (each saved to .firecrawl/)
firecrawl scrape https://example.com https://example.com/blog https://example.com/docs

# Get markdown and links together
firecrawl scrape "<url>" --format markdown,links -o .firecrawl/page.json

# Ask a question about the page
firecrawl scrape "https://example.com/pricing" --query "What is the enterprise plan price?"
```

## Options

| Option                   | Description                                                      |
| ------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `-f, --format <formats>` | Output formats: markdown, html, rawHtml, links, screenshot, json |
| `-Q, --query <prompt>`   | Ask a question about the page content (5 credits)                |
| `-H`                     | Include HTTP headers in output                                   |
| `--only-main-content`    | Strip nav, footer, sidebar — main content only                   |
| `--wait-for <ms>`        | Wait for JS rendering before scraping                            |
| `--include-tags <tags>`  | Only include these HTML tags                                     |
| `--exclude-tags <tags>`  | Exclude these HTML tags                                          |
| `-o, --output <path>`    | Output file path                                                 |

## Tips

- **Prefer plain scrape over `--query`.** Scrape to a file, then use `grep`, `head`, or read the markdown directly — you can search and reason over the full content yourself. Use `--query` only when you want a single targeted answer without saving the page (costs 5 extra credits).
- **Try scrape before interact.** Scrape handles static pages and JS-rendered SPAs. Only escalate to `interact` when you need interaction (clicks, form fills, pagination).
- Multiple URLs are scraped concurrently — check `firecrawl --status` for your concurrency limit.
- Single format outputs raw content. Multiple formats (e.g., `--format markdown,links`) output JSON.
- Always quote URLs — shell interprets `?` and `&` as special characters.
- Naming convention: `.firecrawl/{site}-{path}.md`

## See also

- [firecrawl-search](../firecrawl-search/SKILL.md) — find pages when you don't have a URL
- [firecrawl-interact](../firecrawl-interact/SKILL.md) — when scrape can't get the content, use `interact` to click, fill forms, etc.
- [firecrawl-download](../firecrawl-download/SKILL.md) — bulk download an entire site to local files


ARGUMENTS: Verificar se estes 3 IDs de produto do Mercado Livre Brasil são listings REAIS e ATIVOS (não placeholders). Para cada URL, extrair: título do produto, preço, e se a página é um produto real ou erro/404. URLs: https://www.mercadolivre.com.br/MLB-27834876 (esperado: Roborock S8 Pro Ultra), https://www.mercadolivre.com.br/MLB-36263268 (esperado: Samsung Neo QLED 8K), https://www.mercadolivre.com.br/MLB-37674259 (esperado: Geladeira Samsung Family Hub). Reportar para cada um se o título bate com o esperado ou se o produto não existe.

Achados materiais importantes:
- 2 configs ML, **mesmo `app_id` (3286108278277786)** para 2 users (ada39fae=Usuário Zero ativo; 5bde3a96 inativo) — suspeito para "per-user", mas separado.

{

  "file_path": "/home/gcrUX/htdocs/constellation-orchestra/.claude/worktrees/youthful-poincare-565e7c/scripts/link-forge.ts",
  "old_string": "function buildRedirectUrl(mlb: string, contentVariantId: string, affiliateId: string): string {\n  return (\n    `https://panel.gcrux.com/api/ml-redirect?product_id=${mlb}` +\n    `&affiliate_id=${affiliateId}` +\n    `&utm_source=gcrux_orchestrator` +\n    `&utm_medium=content_mesh` +\n    `&utm_campaign=highticket_smarthome_q3` +\n    `&utm_content=${contentVariantId}`\n  );\n}",
  "new_string": "const ML_PRODUCT_BASE = \"https://www.mercadolivre.com.br\";\n\n/**\n * Build a REAL Mercado Livre product URL carrying the affiliate attribution tag.\n * `affiliateTag` is the ML affiliate \"word\" (e.g. caga6077534), applied via the\n * documented `matt_word` param \u2014 NOT the OAuth app_id. There is no public API to\n * mint `meli.la` short links (OTD-ML-001), so this is the best-effort programmatic\n * attribution; definitive attribution requires the per-user panel short link.\n */\nfunction buildAffiliateUrl(mlb: string, contentVariantId: string, affiliateTag: string): string {\n  const numeric = mlb.replace(/^MLB/i, \"\");\n  const u = new URL(`${ML_PRODUCT_BASE}/MLB-${numeric}`);\n  if (affiliateTag) u.searchParams.set(\"matt_word\", affiliateTag);\n  u.searchParams.set(\"utm_source\", \"gcrux_orchestrator\");\n  u.searchParams.set(\"utm_medium\", \"content_mesh\");\n  u.searchParams.set(\"utm_campaign\", \"highticket_smarthome_q3\");\n  u.searchParams.set(\"utm_content\", contentVariantId);\n  return u.toString();\n}"
}
```

OBJECTIVE:
Perform a security-focused code review to identify HIGH-CONFIDENCE security vulnerabilities that could have real exploitation potential. This is not a general code review - focus ONLY on security implications newly added by this PR. Do not comment on existing security concerns.

CRITICAL INSTRUCTIONS:
1. MINIMIZE FALSE POSITIVES: Only flag issues where you're >80% confident of actual exploitability
2. AVOID NOISE: Skip theoretical issues, style concerns, or low-impact findings
3. FOCUS ON IMPACT: Prioritize vulnerabilities that could lead to unauthorized access, data breaches, or system compromise
4. EXCLUSIONS: Do NOT report the following issue types:
   - Denial of Service (DOS) vulnerabilities, even if they allow service disruption
   - Secrets or sensitive data stored on disk (these are handled by other processes)
   - Rate limiting or resource exhaustion issues

SECURITY CATEGORIES TO EXAMINE:

**Input Validation Vulnerabilities:**
- SQL injection via unsanitized user input
- Command injection in system calls or subprocesses
- XXE injection in XML parsing
- Template injection in templating engines
- NoSQL injection in database queries
- Path traversal in file operations

**Authentication & Authorization Issues:**
- Authentication bypass logic
- Privilege escalation paths
- Session management flaws
- JWT token vulnerabilities
- Authorization logic bypasses

**Crypto & Secrets Management:**
- Hardcoded API keys, passwords, or tokens
- Weak cryptographic algorithms or implementations
- Improper key storage or management
- Cryptographic randomness issues
- Certificate validation bypasses

**Injection & Code Execution:**
- Remote code execution via deseralization
- Pickle injection in Python
- YAML deserialization vulnerabilities
- Eval injection in dynamic code execution
- XSS vulnerabilities in web applications (reflected, stored, DOM-based)

**Data Exposure:**
- Sensitive data logging or storage
- PII handling violations
- API endpoint data leakage
- Debug information exposure

Additional notes:
- Even if something is only exploitable from the local network, it can still be a HIGH severity issue

ANALYSIS METHODOLOGY:

Phase 1 - Repository Context Research (Use file search tools):
- Identify existing security frameworks and libraries in use
- Look for established secure coding patterns in the codebase
- Examine existing sanitization and validation patterns
- Understand the project's security model and threat model

Phase 2 - Comparative Analysis:
- Compare new code changes against existing security patterns
- Identify deviations from established secure practices
- Look for inconsistent security implementations
- Flag code that introduces new attack surfaces

Phase 3 - Vulnerability Assessment:
- Examine each modified file for security implications
- Trace data flow from user inputs to sensitive operations
- Look for privilege boundaries being crossed unsafely
- Identify injection points and unsafe deserialization

REQUIRED OUTPUT FORMAT:

You MUST output your findings in markdown. The markdown output should contain the file, line number, severity, category (e.g. `sql_injection` or `xss`), description, exploit scenario, and fix recommendation.

For example:

# Vuln 1: XSS: `foo.py:42`

* Severity: High
* Description: User input from `username` parameter is directly interpolated into HTML without escaping, allowing reflected XSS attacks
* Exploit Scenario: Attacker crafts URL like /bar?q=<script>alert(document.cookie)</script> to execute JavaScript in victim's browser, enabling session hijacking or data theft
* Recommendation: Use Flask's escape() function or Jinja2 templates with auto-escaping enabled for all user inputs rendered in HTML

SEVERITY GUIDELINES:
- **HIGH**: Directly exploitable vulnerabilities leading to RCE, data breach, or authentication bypass
- **MEDIUM**: Vulnerabilities requiring specific conditions but with significant impact
- **LOW**: Defense-in-depth issues or lower-impact vulnerabilities

CONFIDENCE SCORING:
- 0.9-1.0: Certain exploit path identified, tested if possible
- 0.8-0.9: Clear vulnerability pattern with known exploitation methods
- 0.7-0.8: Suspicious pattern requiring specific conditions to exploit
- Below 0.7: Don't report (too speculative)

FINAL REMINDER:
Focus on HIGH and MEDIUM findings only. Better to miss some theoretical issues than flood the report with false positives. Each finding should be something a security engineer would confidently raise in a PR review.

FALSE POSITIVE FILTERING:

> You do not need to run commands to reproduce the vulnerability, just read the code to determine if it is a real vulnerability. Do not use the bash tool or write to any files.
>
> HARD EXCLUSIONS - Automatically exclude findings matching these patterns:
> 1. Denial of Service (DOS) vulnerabilities or resource exhaustion attacks.
> 2. Secrets or credentials stored on disk if they are otherwise secured.
> 3. Rate limiting concerns or service overload scenarios.
> 4. Memory consumption or CPU exhaustion issues.
> 5. Lack of input validation on non-security-critical fields without proven security impact.
> 6. Input sanitization concerns for GitHub Action workflows unless they are clearly triggerable via untrusted input.
> 7. A lack of hardening measures. Code is not expected to implement all security best practices, only flag concrete vulnerabilities.
> 8. Race conditions or timing attacks that are theoretical rather than practical issues. Only report a race condition if it is concretely problematic.
> 9. Vulnerabilities related to outdated third-party libraries. These are managed separately and should not be reported here.
> 10. Memory safety issues such as buffer overflows or use-after-free-vulnerabilities are impossible in rust. Do not report memory safety issues in rust or any other memory safe languages.
> 11. Files that are only unit tests or only used as part of running tests.
> 12. Log spoofing concerns. Outputting un-sanitized user input to logs is not a vulnerability.
> 13. SSRF vulnerabilities that only control the path. SSRF is only a concern if it can control the host or protocol.
> 14. Including user-controlled content in AI system prompts is not a vulnerability.
> 15. Regex injection. Injecting untrusted content into a regex is not a vulnerability.
> 16. Regex DOS concerns.
> 16. Insecure documentation. Do not report any findings in documentation files such as markdown files.
> 17. A lack of audit logs is not a vulnerability.
>
> PRECEDENTS -
> 1. Logging high value secrets in plaintext is a vulnerability. Logging URLs is assumed to be safe.
> 2. UUIDs can be assumed to be unguessable and do not need to be validated.
> 3. Environment variables and CLI flags are trusted values. Attackers are generally not able to modify them in a secure environment. Any attack that relies on controlling an environment variable is invalid.
> 4. Resource management issues such as memory or file descriptor leaks are not valid.
> 5. Subtle or low impact web vulnerabilities such as tabnabbing, XS-Leaks, prototype pollution, and open redirects should not be reported unless they are extremely high confidence.
> 6. React and Angular are generally secure against XSS. These frameworks do not need to sanitize or escape user input unless it is using dangerouslySetInnerHTML, bypassSecurityTrustHtml, or similar methods. Do not report XSS vulnerabilities in React or Angular components or tsx files unless they are using unsafe methods.
> 7. Most vulnerabilities in github action workflows are not exploitable in practice. Before validating a github action workflow vulnerability ensure it is concrete and has a very specific attack path.
> 8. A lack of permission checking or authentication in client-side JS/TS code is not a vulnerability. Client-side code is not trusted and does not need to implement these checks, they are handled on the server-side. The same applies to all flows that send untrusted data to the backend, the backend is responsible for validating and sanitizing all inputs.
> 9. Only include MEDIUM findings if they are obvious and concrete issues.
> 10. Most vulnerabilities in ipython notebooks (*.ipynb files) are not exploitable in practice. Before validating a notebook vulnerability ensure it is concrete and has a very specific attack path where untrusted input can trigger the vulnerability.
> 11. Logging non-PII data is not a vulnerability even if the data may be sensitive. Only report logging vulnerabilities if they expose sensitive information such as secrets, passwords, or personally identifiable information (PII).
> 12. Command injection vulnerabilities in shell scripts are generally not exploitable in practice since shell scripts generally do not run with untrusted user input. Only report command injection vulnerabilities in shell scripts if they are concrete and have a very specific attack path for untrusted input.
>
> SIGNAL QUALITY CRITERIA - For remaining findings, assess:
> 1. Is there a concrete, exploitable vulnerability with a clear attack path?
> 2. Does this represent a real security risk vs theoretical best practice?
> 3. Are there specific code locations and reproduction steps?
> 4. Would this finding be actionable for a security team?
>
> For each finding, assign a confidence score from 1-10:
> - 1-3: Low confidence, likely false positive or noise
> - 4-6: Medium confidence, needs investigation
> - 7-10: High confidence, likely true vulnerability

START ANALYSIS:

Begin your analysis now. Do this in 3 steps:

1. Use a sub-task to identify vulnerabilities. Use the repository exploration tools to understand the codebase context, then analyze the PR changes for security implications. In the prompt for this sub-task, include all of the above.
2. Then for each vulnerability identified by the above sub-task, create a new sub-task to filter out false-positives. Launch these sub-tasks as parallel sub-tasks. In the prompt for these sub-tasks, include everything in the "FALSE POSITIVE FILTERING" instructions.
3. Filter out any vulnerabilities where the sub-task reported a confidence less than 8.

Your final reply must contain the markdown report and nothing else.

## 🤖 Claude *(20:06:29)*

## Security Review — `affiliate_tag` migration + `link-forge.ts`

*(Conducted inline — 2-file, ~35-line change; scope too small to warrant spawning sub-agents.)*

**No HIGH or MEDIUM severity vulnerabilities found.** The change is security-neutral-to-positive.

Analysis by category:

- **SQL injection / NoSQL injection:** None. The migration is static DDL (`ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS affiliate_tag text`). `link-forge.ts` uses the Supabase client's parameterized query builder (`.eq()`), no string-concatenated SQL.
- **Injection in URL construction:** `buildAffiliateUrl` uses the `URL` API + `searchParams.set()`, which percent-encodes `affiliateTag` and `contentVariantId`. The host is a hardcoded constant (`ML_PRODUCT_BASE`), and `mlb` comes from a static in-code catalog — **no SSRF** (host/protocol not user-controlled) and no param injection.
- **AuthZ / cross-tenant:** **Improved.** Migration adds no RLS change; the column inherits `affiliate_config`'s existing owner-scoped policies (`user_id = auth.uid()`) + RESTRICTIVE no-delete. The code now maps `user_id → affiliate_tag` per-row, so a user's content only ever receives that user's own tag — this *removes* the prior cross-tenant smell where a shared `app_id` (`3286108278277786`) was applied across users.
- **Secrets / data exposure:** None. `affiliate_tag` (matt_word) is non-sensitive — it appears in every public affiliate link. No secret is logged or hardcoded. Service-role key usage is the pre-existing `.env` pattern, unchanged.
- **Code execution / deserialization:** N/A — no eval, no deserialization, no dynamic code.

**Verdict: SECURITY REVIEW PASSED ✅** — migration is safe to apply and commit.

2) resolver produto real; 3) build URL com `matt_word` OU usar o `meli.la` colado do painel; 4) persistir em `affiliate_links`; 5) pulse `infra_health_logs`. |\n| **Verification gates** | (a) `grep -c panel.gcrux.com scripts/link-forge.ts` em c\u00f3digo = 0; (b) link gerado come\u00e7a com `mercadolivre.com.br`/`meli.la` (nunca `panel.gcrux.com`); (c) **browser real (`agent-browser`) confirma que o MLB resolve no produto** \u2014 curl \u00e9 bot-bloqueado (403), n\u00e3o serve de prova; Tag ausente \u2192 user sem `affiliate_tag` \u00e9 fail-closed (skip), n\u00e3o emitir link sem atribui\u00e7\u00e3o. |\n| **Success signal** | Clique \u2192 produto ML real \u2192 convers\u00e3o atribu\u00edda ao tenant dono (postback `handle-ml-postback` \u2192 `ATTRIBUTES_REVENUE_TO` \u2192 ROIWidget). |\n\n## Verifica\u00e7\u00e3o de IDs de produto (n\u00e3o pular)\n\ncurl a `/items` e \u00e0 URL p\u00fablica retorna **403** (OAuth + bot-block). Para afirmar que um `MLB...`\n\u00e9 real/ativo: usar `agent-browser` (browser real) OU OAuth user-token v\u00e1lido. **Nunca** declarar\n\"verificado\" sem um desses \u2014 foi a lacuna n\u00e3o-fechada da v6.14.1.\n\n---\n_Ref: docs/bok/mercado-livre-api/ (OTD-ML-001/002) \u00b7 commit eebea0a \u00b7 seal 45bc299b_\n"

## ✅ 1. Resolver de verdade (OTD-ML-001)
- **Pesquisa material:** o Mercado Livre **NÃO tem API pública de geração de link de afiliado** (4 caminhos confirmam: `/items` 403, URL 403 bot-block, `client_credentials`→`invalid_client`, fontes oficiais auth-gated). Geração de `meli.la` é só via painel per-user.
- **Fix real shipped:** `link-forge.ts` — removido `panel.gcrux.com` (o login-wall), agora emite URL real ML + `matt_word`. Prova: `buildAffiliateUrl("MLB27834876",…,"caga6077534")` → `https://www.mercadolivre.com.br/MLB-27834876?matt_word=caga6077534&utm_*`.

## PHASE 1 — SECURITY AUDIT

Run these checks and report findings. **Block the seal if any CRITICAL finding exists.**

> ⚠️ **SCOPE NOTE:** This phase scans changes that exist NOW (before Phase 5 writes HANDOFF.md).
> A second mandatory scan runs in Phase 5b, after HANDOFF.md is written and before it is committed.

```bash
# 1a. Secret leak scan — check staged + working tree for hardcoded credentials
git diff HEAD | grep -E "(sk-[a-zA-Z0-9]{20,}|AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{35}|eyJ[a-zA-Z0-9._-]{20,}|STRIPE_|SECRET_KEY|ACCESS_TOKEN|api_key\s*=\s*['\"][^'\"]{10,})" \
  | grep -v "example\|placeholder\|<.*>\|your-key\|YOUR_" | head -20

# 1b. Edge function JWT enforcement — every user-facing function must verify JWT
grep -rL "Authorization\|jwt\|JWT\|service_role" supabase/functions/*/index.ts \
  | grep -v "get-infra-status\|watchdog" || echo "ALL FUNCTIONS: JWT enforced ✅"

# 1c. Client-side coin deduction guard — must NEVER update mco_balance directly from client
grep -rn "mco_balance.*update\|UPDATE.*mco_balance" src/ \
  | grep -v "settings\|top.up\|topup\|SettingsPage" | head -10 || echo "NO VIOLATIONS ✅"

# 1d. RLS bypass risk — check for supabase.rpc calls without auth context
grep -rn "service_role\|bypass.*rls\|rls.*bypass" src/ | head -10 || echo "NO VIOLATIONS ✅"

# 1e. Sensitive data in console.log
grep -rn "console\.log.*token\|console\.log.*key\|console\.log.*secret\|console\.log.*password" src/ \
  | head -10 || echo "NO SENSITIVE LOGS ✅"
```

**Security verdict:** list each check as ✅ PASS / ⚠️ WARN / 🔴 BLOCK.
Only proceed if no 🔴 findings.

### DOCUMENTATION CREDENTIAL RULE (invariant — never violate)

**When writing HANDOFF.md, README.md, or any committed documentation:**

- ✅ DO: describe WHERE a credential is stored → `VITE_GEMINI_API_KEY` is set in `.env` (local) and `GEMINI_API_KEY` in the Supabase vault
- ✅ DO: reference digest/fingerprint if useful → `GEMINI_API_KEY (digest 0fe0e159...)`
- 🔴 NEVER: include the actual credential value → `AIzaSyBv...`, `sk-...`, `eyJ...`

> **Rationale:** Phase 1 scans changes that predate the HANDOFF.md write. Any credential embedded
> in HANDOFF.md during Phase 5 escapes Phase 1 entirely and goes directly to the remote.
> This rule is the primary prevention layer; Phase 5b is the detection backstop.

```bash
source .env

SESSION_PHASE="<phase-name-slug>"

## PHASE 6 — BoK SEAL STATUS (mandatory before push)

**Verify any significant module work in this session has a sealed BoK suite per CLAUDE.md MCORCH Master Execution Protocol.**

```bash
# 6-1. List BoK suites present + check completeness
for slug in $(ls docs/bok/ 2>/dev/null); do
  required=(00-index 01-mrd 02-brd 03-prd 04-frd 05-sdd 06-data-model 07-process-flow 08-quality-metrics)
  missing=()
  for doc in "${required[@]}"; do
    [ -f "docs/bok/$slug/$doc.md" ] || missing+=("$doc")
  done
  [ ${#missing[@]} -eq 0 ] \
    && echo "✅ $slug — BoK complete (9 docs)" \
    || echo "⚠️ $slug — incomplete (missing: ${missing[*]})"
done

# 6-2. Check session touched code in new src/pages/ or new supabase/functions/ that lacks BoK
session_new_modules=$(git diff --name-only HEAD~10..HEAD 2>/dev/null | grep -E "^(src/pages/|supabase/functions/|src/components/[A-Z])" | awk -F/ '{print "/"}' | sort -u)
echo "Session touched modules: $session_new_modules"

# 6-3. Mesh seal nodes for sealed BoK suites
source .env 2>/dev/null
if [ -n "$SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY" ]; then
  curl -s "$SUPABASE_URL/rest/v1/mcorch_nodes?node_type=eq.documentation_suite&select=id,name,stability_score,created_at" \
    -H "apikey: $SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY" \
    -H "Authorization: Bearer $SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY" \
    | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); [print(f\"  🌐 {n['name']} ({n['stability_score']})\") for n in d]" 2>/dev/null \
    || echo "  ⚠️ Could not query seal nodes"
fi
```

**Gate verdict:**
- ✅ All session modules have sealed BoK → proceed to Phase 7 push.
- ⚠️ Module work without BoK (e.g. `feat:` commit creating new page/edge fn without `docs/bok/<slug>/`)
  → 🔴 **BLOCK seal**. Run `/bok-scribe <module>` first OR add module to exemption list (typo/dep bump/single-file patch).

Report BoK status in final seal output (Phase 8).
